# Social Studies course — titles, FROM THE BOTTOM

Social Studies is one world course: the Earth, its peoples and
their past are the same everywhere; only the way of teaching
differs, and the way shows inside as examples. The first stocked
part is built from Ethiopia's Social Studies books for Grades 7
and 8. The path climbs from the learner's own ground outward —
the planet under their feet, the people on it, the past behind
them, and the world they are growing into.

**First Steps → Earth and Space → Population, Transport and Maps
→ People and Resources → Ethiopia: Ancient and Medieval → Africa
to 1500 → The Ancient World → The Land and Air → Culture and
Work → Resources and Conflict → Modern Ethiopia → Africa after
1500 → The Modern World → Global Issues → Microeconomics →
Market and Money → Macroeconomics → Ethiopia and Development**

That ladder IS the path order (recommended, never forced — a
learner can jump in higher; the "start here instead" path points
down this ladder). Inside the course the organisation is by
**levels, not grades**: both school years' lessons sit inside
the one title at rising depth wherever a title spans both books
(as in Global Issues).

## The titles

- `first-steps/` — what Social Studies is, our neighbourhood,
  my house, my home, my town, my district and my village,
  Ibn Battuta — traveller and explorer, teaching Geography
  to beginning readers, and how this course
  works (the books' own way: geography →
  history → today, activity first).
- `earth-and-space/` — the universe, the solar system, the
  Earth: shape, size, movements, seasons, time (including
  the Moon and its phases).
- `population-transport-and-maps/` — people on the Earth:
  distribution, movement, transport, and reading maps
  (including colouring Suriname's map and flag, Argentina's
  population today, thematic maps of Mercosur integration, a
  day on the border, Iceland's administrative districts and
  voyaging and settling the Pacific islands, movement
  — people, goods and ideas, and Morocco's map, borders and
  cities, drawing a relief map of Morocco, Morocco's
  population distribution and Morocco's transport networks,
  no entry — borders and excluded spaces, stop dreaming
  of a car — energy and transport, Sweden on the map,
  living in Sweden — town and country, and kinds of houses).
- `people-and-resources/` — settlement, natural resources,
  economic activities, and looking after the land (including
  quality of life — Argentina and Latin America compared,
  Mercosur economic integration, kinds of integration, the
  South American gas pipeline database, India's growing
  economy, Moroccan agriculture and livestock, Morocco's two
  sea fronts and ports, Morocco's minerals and the
  phosphate industry, Brazil could be different — territory,
  and regional blocs — does union make strength?).
- `ethiopia-ancient-and-medieval/` — Ethiopia from the earliest
  times through the medieval kingdoms and sultanates.
- `africa-to-1500/` — Africa's peoples, states and trade to
  about 1500 (including Islam in West Africa, and Ethiopia —
  kingdoms and faiths before 1800, and Southern Africa's
  first peoples — San, Khoikhoi and Bantu, and Gabon —
  first peoples and migrations, and the Bantu migrations).
- `ancient-world/` — the ancient civilisations and the medieval
  world beyond Africa (including Ibn Battuta — traveller
  of the Islamic world, and the Arabian Peninsula —
  cradle of civilisations, and the Arabian Peninsula
  before Islam, and Morocco — prehistoric Morocco,
  Amazigh civilisation, Roman Morocco and Aedemon,
  the Idrisids and the Qarawiyyin, the Almoravids,
  the Almohads, and the Marinids and their madrasas, and
  Myanmar — Myanmar prehistory, the Pyu city-states,
  the Bagan Empire, and hunters and first farmers, and
  Ukraine — Ukraine enters the Middle Ages, the Slavs
  settle and found Kyiv, Rus-Ukraine — a state is born,
  Rus-Ukraine under Oleh and Ihor, Rus-Ukraine under
  Olha and Sviatoslav, Volodymyr baptises Rus-Ukraine,
  Yaroslav the Wise and the Ruska Pravda, Rus-Ukraine —
  how the land was ruled and lived, culture of
  Rus-Ukraine — the golden age, Rus-Ukraine breaks
  apart — the Yaroslavychi, Volodymyr Monomakh holds
  Rus together, Rus-Ukraine fragmented — Kyiv,
  Chernihiv and Pereiaslav, Halych and Volyn rise,
  culture of Rus-Ukraine — the age of fragmentation,
  Roman Mstyslavych founds the Halych-Volyn state, the
  Mongols invade Rus-Ukraine, Danylo, king of Rus, the
  Halych-Volyn state after Danylo, culture of the
  Halych-Volyn land, Ukrainian lands in the Lithuanian
  state, Ukrainian lands under Hungary, Moldova and
  Muscovy, Crimea and the northern Black Sea in the
  late Middle Ages, Ukrainian towns, crafts and
  Magdeburg law, Ukrainian culture in the 14th and
  15th centuries, and Ukraine in medieval Europe, and
  Haiti — the Taïno of Quisqueya, prehistoric art —
  cave paintings and sculpture, and Caribbean first
  peoples — fishers and farmers, and Costa Rica — the Inca — the Andean civilisation, Costa Rica's indigenous peoples before the Spanish, and medieval cities, trade, universities and plague).
- `the-land-and-air/` — continents and oceans, the forces that
  shape the land, the atmosphere, weather and climate, map work
  (including the UAE's location and emirates map and desert
  wildlife of the UAE, Zambia's agro-ecological regions and
  climate and agro-ecological regions, reading a district
  boundary map of Iceland, India — landscape and monsoon
  climate, hot-spot islands, how coral atolls form and high
  islands and low islands of Micronesia, place — what
  makes a place, the Arabian Peninsula — location and
  land, Morocco's relief — mountains, plateaus and plains,
  Morocco's climate — temperature and rainfall, Morocco's
  rivers and dams, producing and consuming space, Sweden's
  nature, naming Sweden's plants and animals, warm and
  cold climates, and Sweden's climate — a long country).
- `culture-and-work/` — culture, economic activities, trade,
  and tax (including agriculture in Zambia's agro-ecological
  regions, festivals of the world's religions, sacred texts,
  food and clothing of religions, religion and caste in
  India, is industry over?, and shopping malls and
  consumption space).
- `resources-and-conflict/` — natural resources, their care,
  and conflict over resources (including environmental hazards
  and disasters, catastrophes — avoidable or inevitable?,
  agrochemicals — do you take poison?, Ukraine on the map,
  Russia on the map, and NATO countries in Europe 2022).
- `modern-ethiopia/` — Ethiopia from 1855 to today. (Student
  book alone — no teacher evidence yet; the Grade 8 teacher
  guide's Units 4–7 are still owed.)
- `africa-after-1500/` — Africa from 1500: trade, colonisation,
  resistance, independence (including Zambia — primary
  resistance in Northern Rhodesia, secondary resistance and
  the road to independence, liberation struggle sites, and
  nationalists of the independence struggle, and Cabo Verde —
  discovery and settlement, a creole society is born, on the
  world routes, decline and Porto Grande, popular revolts,
  end of the slave society, liberation and independence, and
  the two Republics, and problems of independent West
  African states, women and authority in West African
  history, and the environment in West African history, and
  East African coast — Portuguese conquest and rule,
  Buganda — kingdom, clans, Kabaka and trade, Buganda —
  religious wars and British occupation, Ethiopia — Era
  of the Princes (Zemene Mesafint), Ethiopia — Tewodros II
  the unifier, Ethiopia — Yohannes IV against Egypt and
  Italy, Northern Nguni revolution before Shaka, Mfecane —
  new states: Moshoeshoe, Mzilikazi and the Ngoni, Cape
  Colony and the frontier wars, and South Africa —
  diamonds, gold and the road to union, and Gabon —
  France in Gabon and the founding of Libreville,
  independence, 1960, daily life changes, Brazza and
  the exploration of the Ogooué, Gabon in French Congo
  and the AÉF, and Gabon — resistance to colonisation).
  (No teacher evidence yet.)
- `modern-world/` — the early-modern and modern world
  (including Guyana — our nation: independence and symbols,
  Canada's whaling story: Inuit whaling — the traditional
  hunt, Whalers from across the sea, and Whaling today —
  protecting the whales, United Arab Emirates — the
  union and Sheikh Zayed, and Argentina — building the
  state 1860–1916, the great immigration, and the
  conquest of indigenous territories, and Timor-Leste —
  the Carnation Revolution and the proclamation of
  1975, the Indonesian invasion and the war 1975–1979,
  the resistance reorganises: Falintil and the 1983
  ceasefire, the Church and the diplomatic front, the
  Santa Cruz massacre 1991, the clandestine resistance,
  the referendum of 1999 and the restoration of 2002,
  and heroes and memory of the resistance, and Saudi
  Arabia — Founding Day and National Day, and
  unification under Abdulaziz: the brave courier, and
  Morocco — the Saadians and El Badi Palace, and the
  Alaouites and the kasbahs, and Brazil — from slavery
  to free labour, urbanisation and industrialisation,
  Paraná urbanisation and the Port of Paranaguá, state
  power and violence, imperialism and its crisis,
  domination and resistance in the modern West, and
  contemporary social movements, and Switzerland —
  compulsory welfare measures: care or coercion?, the
  Albin family dissolved, Uschi Waser and the Yenish
  Children of the Road, Cornelia Studer and the Gott
  hilft children's home, Florian Branger and
  administrative detention, and Ruedi Hofer, the
  Verdingkinder and reparation, and the United States — its own national survey in 27 lessons, from the Americas before 1492 to America in the twenty-first century, and Haiti — forts built to defend independence, the flag and 18 May 1803, slavery and the plantation economy of Saint-Domingue, the Code Noir — the law that ruled slavery, resistance, marronage and revolt in Saint-Domingue, the Haitian Revolution, 1791–1804, building the Haitian state, 1804–1843, independence debt and neocolonialism by debt, and the US occupation of Haiti, 1915–1934, and Costa Rica — the Spanish conquest of America and Costa Rica, colonial Costa Rica — institutions and daily life, colonial Costa Rica — exploitation and heritage, absolute monarchies and state formation, Latin American independence — ideas and processes, Costa Rica — building the nation-state, 1821–1848, the coffee state and consolidation, 1850–1890, the coffee and banana agro-export model, daily life in the late 19th century, Cold War social and political struggles, Cold War economic models, the end of 'real socialism' and the Cold War, China in a multipolar world, post-Cold War geopolitical tensions, ethnic cleansing and human rights, Latin America in contemporary geopolitics, the agro-export model in crisis, democracy and social struggles, 1900–1948, 1948: social reform and civil war, import substitution, 1950–1980, the welfare state, 1950–1980, the electoral system after 1949, the 1980s crisis and export promotion, state and social movements since 1980, and the women's suffrage struggle).
  (No teacher evidence yet.)
- `global-issues/` — today's shared problems: population
  pressure, HIV/AIDS, climate, water, forests — and protecting
  the environment where you live (including India's
  environmental problems, environmental problems in
  Morocco, networks — we of the network, are humans
  rational — and the environment?, sorting and recycling
  waste, and choices that help the climate). (Grade 8 part: no teacher
  evidence yet.)
